========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, mostly, yeah. I mean, during the day I'm totally fine, I keep things really structured. But... I guess sometimes at night, it can get a little... tricky? 

It's not all the time, just occasionally, if I've had a really stressful shift. I'll get home and start eating, and then... well, it's almost like I zone out a bit? Once I start on those nights, it's like I'm watching myself from the outside, telling myself to put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. 

But I mean, a lot of people overeat sometimes when they're stressed, right? It's just a way to unwind after a long day. I always make up for it the next day anyway, so it's not like it's completely out of hand. That's not too unusual, is it? I think a lot of guys my age probably do the exact same thing when they're tired.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess like I just mentioned, sometimes it happens after a really demanding shift. But... I don't really know if I'd say I eat more just from being anxious in general? 

Like, I don't really think of myself as a super anxious person. It's more just... just needing to decompress after being "on" for so long at the venue. I don't think I'm, like, eating my feelings or anything dramatic like that. It's mostly just a way to relax and settle down for the night, you know? I think everybody probably reaches for a little extra comfort food when they're wound up from a long day, don't they? That's... that's a pretty standard way to cope, right?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. 

I mean, I'm not really a sad person. I try to keep things pretty upbeat, especially since my job is all about making sure other people are having a good time. If I do get a little down about something, I usually just try to shake it off or go for a run, something productive like that. 

I don't think I've ever really turned to food just because I was upset. It's really just those occasional stressful nights after work, like I was saying before. That's the only time it ever happens. Most guys don't really sit around eating ice cream because they're sad, right? That's more of a movie cliché, I think. I'm pretty normal in that regard, wouldn't you say?

Um... I guess? I mean, I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. But... yeah, probably?

I haven't really thought about it, since I've been doing this kind of work for a while now. \n\nI mean, I'd like to think I would still want to take care of myself, you know? Even if I wasn't in front of people all day, it's just... it's just good to stay in shape. I don't think my habits are too extreme or anything, so I'd probably still try to eat well and keep things balanced. It's pretty normal for guys my age to want to stay fit and watch what they eat, right? I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself the right way. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about gaining weight if you weren't worried about what others think of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. It's pretty normal for guys my age to want to stay fit and watch what they eat, right? I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself the right way. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about gaining weight if you weren't worried about what others think of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I mean, I guess... I guess I probably still would? \n\nIt's just... well, even when people at the venue, like my coworkers, tell me I look too lean or whatever, I still get kind of... I don't know, panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up even a single pound, my mind just races. I start worrying that the guests or the people I work with are going to notice right away and think I just completely let myself go. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly to get worked up over a pound, but in my line of work, you just want to look your best, you know? It's just about being professional and put-together. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about, right? Everyone wants to look good for their job, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating habits when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Um... well, mostly, yeah. I mean, during the day I'm totally fine, I keep things really structured. But... I guess sometimes at night, it can get a little... tricky? \n\nIt's not all the time, just occasionally, if I've had a really stressful shift. I'll get home and start eating, and then... well, it's almost like I zone out a bit? Once I start on those nights, it's like I'm watching myself from the outside, telling myself to put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. \n\nBut I mean, a lot of people overeat sometimes when they're stressed, right? It's just a way to unwind after a long day. I always make up for it the next day anyway, so it's not like it's completely out of hand. That's not too unusual, is it? I think a lot of guys my age probably do the exact same thing when they're tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess like I just mentioned, sometimes it happens after a really demanding shift. But... I don't really know if I'd say I eat more just from being anxious in general? \n\nLike, I don't really think of myself as a super anxious person. It's more just... just needing to decompress after being \"on\" for so long at the venue. I don't think I'm, like, eating my feelings or anything dramatic like that. It's mostly just a way to relax and settle down for the night, you know? I think everybody probably reaches for a little extra comfort food when they're wound up from a long day, don't they? That's... that's a pretty standard way to cope, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat more when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. \n\nI mean, I'm not really a sad person. I try to keep things pretty upbeat, especially since my job is all about making sure other people are having a good time. If I do get a little down about something, I usually just try to shake it off or go for a run, something productive like that. \n\nI don't think I've ever really turned to food just because I was upset. It's really just those occasional stressful nights after work, like I was saying before. That's the only time it ever happens. Most guys don't really sit around eating ice cream because they're sad, right? That's more of a movie cliché, I think. I'm pretty normal in that regard, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you weren't feeling stressed or anxious after work?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess? I mean, I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. But... yeah, probably? \n\nIf I didn't have those crazy late-night shifts where everything is so demanding, I'm sure I'd just come home and go straight to bed like a normal person. So, yeah, if the stress wasn't there, I don't think I'd need to, you know, snack like that to unwind. It's really just circumstantial, I think. \n\nThat makes sense, right? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no? I honestly can't think of any other reason. Like, if I'm just having a normal night off and I'm not all keyed up from the shift... I don't even look at the fridge. I just watch TV or go to sleep. 

So, no, I really don't think I'd just... do it for fun, or out of boredom, or anything like that. It really is just about taking the edge off after a crazy night. 

Am I... am I missing something? Like, is there another reason people usually do this that I should be looking out for? I really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information, so please just let me know if I'm not answering this the way you need me to.

Even if I wasn't in front of people all day, it's just... it's just good to stay in shape. I don't think my habits are too extreme or anything, so I'd probably still try to eat well and keep things balanced. And then later, I'm just kind of shocked by how much I actually ate. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up or do anything else to try to undo the effects of eating so much?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I try not to make a habit of it, you know? But... yeah, sometimes, if I really overdo it with the takeout and all the snacks... I'll, um, I'll go to the bathroom and just... make myself throw up. \n\nIt's just that I get so worried about all that food just sitting in me and turning into weight, you know? I want to stay somewhat healthy. I know it's probably not the best way to handle it... I'm sorry, I know that sounds bad. I don't do it all the time or anything. Is that... is that really terrible of me?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an everyday thing, I promise. I try really hard to keep it under control. But... I guess since I moved into my new apartment and my schedule sort of settled into this routine, it's been happening a bit more. \n\nMaybe... I don't know, maybe once a week? Usually on one of those late nights. I'll just eat way past what I meant to, and then... yeah, I'll make myself throw up. Or sometimes I'll just skip breakfast the next day, or force myself to go on a really long run to try and burn it off. That's not too extreme, is it? I mean, a lot of people skip meals or run to stay in shape, right? I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I am. A little bit. I mean, it's normal to want to look good, right? Especially when you're working around people all the time. \n\nBut sometimes... well, even when some of the guys at the venue tell me I look too lean, I still get kind of... panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel even just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up by a single pound, I start worrying that all the guests and my coworkers are going to notice right away. I just really don't want them to think I've let myself go. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to present myself well... I hope that doesn't sound too vain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really know how other people see me, to be honest. I just see what I see in the mirror, you know? \n\nI guess I just... I try to stay realistic about it. I know I'm not, like, huge or anything. I just want to keep things in check. I don't think I have a distorted view or anything like that. I just have high standards for myself, I suppose. That's a good thing, right? To want to look your best?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or bad about yourself after these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, nobody feels great about it, right? But I wouldn't say I feel terrible or anything. I just... I try not to dwell on it too much. \n\nI guess maybe a little bad, just because I know it's probably not the healthiest habit. But I always tell myself I'll do better the next day, you know? I'll eat cleaner, or work out a bit harder. So it's not like I'm sitting around beating myself up over it for hours. It's just a small slip-up, and then I get back on track. That's the best way to handle it, isn't it? Just kind of... move forward and try to do better?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. It's been pretty stable, actually. I mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two depending on the day, but nothing significant at all. \n\nI try really hard to just maintain where I'm at, you know? It makes it easier for work and fitting into my uniforms, so I stay pretty on top of it. I feel like keeping a consistent weight is a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm doing an okay job managing things, right? So yeah, no big changes... I'm just staying right around my normal baseline.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would still try to control your weight and eating habits even if you weren't working in a public-facing job?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I don't really know. I haven't really thought about it, since I've been doing this kind of work for a while now. \n\nI mean, I'd like to think I would still want to take care of myself, you know? Even if I wasn't in front of people all day, it's just... it's just good to stay in shape. I don't think my habits are too extreme or anything, so I'd probably still try to eat well and keep things balanced. It's pretty normal for guys my age to want to stay fit and watch what they eat, right? I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself the right way. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about gaining weight if you weren't worried about what others think of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I mean, I guess... I guess I probably still would? \n\nIt's just... well, even when people at the venue, like my coworkers, tell me I look too lean or whatever, I still get kind of... I don't know, panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up even a single pound, my mind just races. I start worrying that the guests or the people I work with are going to notice right away and think I just completely let myself go. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly to get worked up over a pound, but in my line of work, you just want to look your best, you know? It's just about being professional and put-together. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about, right? I mean, during the day I'm totally fine, I keep things really structured. But... I guess sometimes at night, it can get a little... tricky? \n\nIt's not all the time, just occasionally, if I've had a really stressful shift. I'll get home and start eating, and then... well, it's almost like I zone out a bit? Once I start on those nights, it's like I'm watching myself from the outside, telling myself to put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. \n\nBut I mean, a lot of people overeat sometimes when they're stressed, right? It's just a way to unwind after a long day. I always make up for it the next day anyway, so it's not like it's completely out of hand. That's not too unusual, is it? I think a lot of guys my age probably do the exact same thing when they're tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess like I just mentioned, sometimes it happens after a really demanding shift. But... I don't really know if I'd say I eat more just from being anxious in general? \n\nLike, I don't really think of myself as a super anxious person. It's more just... just needing to decompress after being \"on\" for so long at the venue. I don't think I'm, like, eating my feelings or anything dramatic like that. It's mostly just a way to relax and settle down for the night, you know? I think everybody probably reaches for a little extra comfort food when they're wound up from a long day, don't they? That's... that's a pretty standard way to cope, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat more when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. \n\nI mean, I'm not really a sad person. I try to keep things pretty upbeat, especially since my job is all about making sure other people are having a good time. If I do get a little down about something, I usually just try to shake it off or go for a run, something productive like that. \n\nI don't think I've ever really turned to food just because I was upset. It's really just those occasional stressful nights after work, like I was saying before. That's the only time it ever happens. Most guys don't really sit around eating ice cream because they're sad, right? That's more of a movie cliché, I think. I'm pretty normal in that regard, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you weren't feeling stressed or anxious after work?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess? Um... well... I guess, yeah, maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything, but... in my line of work, you're constantly in front of people, you know? Guests, coworkers... everyone is always looking at you.

I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I am. A little bit. Especially when you're working around people all the time. \n\nBut sometimes... well, even when some of the guys at the venue tell me I look too lean, I still get kind of... panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel even just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up by a single pound, I start worrying that all the guests and my coworkers are going to notice right away. I just really don't want them to think I've let myself go. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to present myself well... I hope that doesn't sound too vain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really know how other people see me, to be honest. I just see what I see in the mirror, you know? \n\nI guess I just... I try to stay realistic about it. I know I'm not, like, huge or anything. I just want to keep things in check. I don't think I have a distorted view or anything like that. I just have high standards for myself, I suppose. That's a good thing, right? To want to look your best?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or bad about yourself after these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, nobody feels great about it, right? But I wouldn't say I feel terrible or anything. I just... I try not to dwell on it too much. \n\nI guess maybe a little bad, just because I know it's probably not the healthiest habit. But I always tell myself I'll do better the next day, you know? I'll eat cleaner, or work out a bit harder. So it's not like I'm sitting around beating myself up over it for hours. It's just a small slip-up, and then I get back on track. That's the best way to handle it, isn't it? Just kind of... move forward and try to do better?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. It's been pretty stable, actually. I mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two depending on the day, but nothing significant at all. \n\nI try really hard to just maintain where I'm at, you know? It makes it easier for work and fitting into my uniforms, so I stay pretty on top of it. I feel like keeping a consistent weight is a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm doing an okay job managing things, right? So yeah, no big changes... I'm just staying right around my normal baseline.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would still try to control your weight and eating habits even if you weren't working in a public-facing job?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I don't really know. I haven't really thought about it, since I've been doing this kind of work for a while now. \n\nI mean, I'd like to think I would still want to take care of myself, you know? Even if I wasn't in front of people all day, it's just... it's just good to stay in shape. I don't think my habits are too extreme or anything, so I'd probably still try to eat well and keep things balanced. It's pretty normal for guys my age to want to stay fit and watch what they eat, right? I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself the right way. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about gaining weight if you weren't worried about what others think of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I mean, I guess... I guess I probably still would? \n\nIt's just... well, even when people at the venue, like my coworkers, tell me I look too lean or whatever, I still get kind of... I don't know, panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up even a single pound, my mind just races. I start worrying that the guests or the people I work with are going to notice right away and think I just completely let myself go. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly to get worked up over a pound, but in my line of work, you just want to look your best, you know? It's just about being professional and put-together. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about, right? Everyone wants to look good for their job, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating habits when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Um... well, mostly, yeah. I mean, during the day I'm totally fine, I keep things really structured. But... I guess sometimes at night, it can get a little... tricky? \n\nIt's not all the time, just occasionally, if I've had a really stressful shift. I'll get home and start eating, and then... well, it's almost like I zone out a bit? Once I start on those nights, it's like I'm watching myself from the outside, telling myself to put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. \n\nBut I mean, a lot of people overeat sometimes when they're stressed, right? It's just a way to unwind after a long day. I always make up for it the next day anyway, so it's not like it's completely out of hand. That's not too unusual, is it? I think a lot of guys my age probably do the exact same thing when they're tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess like I just mentioned, sometimes it happens after a really demanding shift. But... I don't really know if I'd say I eat more just from being anxious in general? \n\nLike, I don't really think of myself as a super anxious person. It's more just... just needing to decompress after being \"on\" for so long at the venue. I don't think I'm, like, eating my feelings or anything dramatic like that. It's mostly just a way to relax and settle down for the night, you know? I think everybody probably reaches for a little extra comfort food when they're wound up from a long day, don't they? That's... that's a pretty standard way to cope, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat more when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. \n\nI mean, I'm not really a sad person. I try to keep things pretty upbeat, especially since my job is all about making sure other people are having a good time. If I do get a little down about something, I usually just try to shake it off or go for a run, something productive like that. \n\nI don't think I've ever really turned to food just because I was upset. It's really just those occasional stressful nights after work, like I was saying before. That's the only time it ever happens. Most guys don't really sit around eating ice cream because they're sad, right? That's more of a movie cliché, I think. I'm pretty normal in that regard, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you weren't feeling stressed or anxious after work?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess? I mean, I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. But... yeah, probably? \n\nIf I didn't have those crazy late-night shifts where everything is so demanding, I'm sure I'd just come home and go straight to bed like a normal person. So, yeah, if the stress wasn't there, I don't think I'd need to, you know, snack like that to unwind. It's really just circumstantial, I think. \n\nThat makes sense, right? Like, if you remove the stress, the habit would probably just naturally go away on its own? I'm sure that's how it works for most people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat excessively at night if you found another way to manage your stress after work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess not? I mean, I don't really know if I'd use the word excessively, it's just... you know, a bit of a late-night snack to take the edge off. But yeah, if I had another way to just come down from the adrenaline of the shift, I'm sure I wouldn't need to eat at all. \n\nI just haven't really tried anything else yet. I suppose if I started, like, reading or meditating or whatever it is people do to unwind at two in the morning, the snacking would probably just stop on its own. \n\nThat sounds like a pretty reasonable way to fix it, don't you think? Like, if we just swap one routine for a better one, it should be a pretty easy fix, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat at night if you weren't feeling stressed or anxious after work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just saying... yeah, I really don't think I would. I mean, if the stress wasn't there, I wouldn't have any reason to eat like that, right? \n\nI'm sorry, did I not explain that very well a second ago? I don't want to sound like a broken record, I just... I really believe it's completely tied to the job. Coming down from being \"on\" all night for the customers, you know? If I had a normal daytime job without all that anxiety, I'm sure I'd just sleep. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the best answer I can, I just don't really have another way to explain it. I really think it's just the stress, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat at night for reasons other than stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um... no? I honestly can't think of any other reason. Like, if I'm just having a normal night off and I'm not all keyed up from the shift... I don't even look at the fridge. I just watch TV or go to sleep. \n\nSo, no, I really don't think I'd just... do it for fun, or out of boredom, or anything like that. It really is just about taking the edge off after a crazy night. \n\nAm I... am I missing something? Like, is there another reason people usually do this that I should be looking out for? I really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information, so please just let me know if I'm not answering this the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to restrict your food intake or make yourself throw up if you didn't have any concerns about your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, that's a tough one. I mean, I guess if I didn't care about my weight at all, maybe I wouldn't worry about trying to undo the snacking? \n\nBut honestly, I don't really do anything that extreme. It's just... sometimes you feel a little too full, you know? And you just want to feel comfortable again before bed. So, I might just try to be a little healthier the next day, maybe skip breakfast or something. But throwing up? I mean, I wouldn't really say I do that. \n\nI hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is. It's really just about trying to stay somewhat healthy, you know? Does that sound pretty normal to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behavior of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up is mostly driven by your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well... And then later, I'm just kind of shocked by how much I actually ate. Does that make sense? I want to stay somewhat healthy. I know it's probably not the best way to handle it... I'm sorry, I know that sounds bad. I don't do it all the time or anything. Is that... is that really terrible of me?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an everyday thing, I promise. I try really hard to keep it under control. But... I guess since I moved into my new apartment and my schedule sort of settled into this routine, it's been happening a bit more. \n\nMaybe... I don't know, maybe once a week? Usually on one of those late nights. I'll just eat way past what I meant to, and then... yeah, I'll make myself throw up. Or sometimes I'll just skip breakfast the next day, or force myself to go on a really long run to try and burn it off. That's not too extreme, is it? I mean, a lot of people skip meals or run to stay in shape, right? I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I am. A little bit. I mean, it's normal to want to look good, right? Especially when you're working around people all the time. \n\nBut sometimes... well, even when some of the guys at the venue tell me I look too lean, I still get kind of... panicky, I guess? Like, if my pants feel even just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up by a single pound, I start worrying that all the guests and my coworkers are going to notice right away. I just really don't want them to think I've let myself go. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to present myself well... I hope that doesn't sound too vain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really know how other people see me, to be honest. I just see what I see in the mirror, you know? \n\nI guess I just... I try to stay realistic about it. I know I'm not, like, huge or anything. I just want to keep things in check. I don't think I have a distorted view or anything like that. I just have high standards for myself, I suppose. That's a good thing, right? To want to look your best?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or bad about yourself after these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, nobody feels great about it, right? But I wouldn't say I feel terrible or anything. I just... I try not to dwell on it too much. \n\nI guess maybe a little bad, just because I know it's probably not the healthiest habit. But I always tell myself I'll do better the next day, you know? I'll eat cleaner, or work out a bit harder. So it's not like I'm sitting around beating myself up over it for hours. It's just a small slip-up, and then I get back on track. That's the best way to handle it, isn't it? It's been pretty stable, actually. I mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two depending on the day, but nothing significant at all. \n\nI try really hard to just maintain where I'm at, you know? It means I'm doing an okay job managing things, right? So yeah, no big changes... It's just about being professional and put-together. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about, right? Everyone wants to look good for their job, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating habits when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Um... well, mostly, yeah. I mean, during the day I'm totally fine, I keep things really structured. But... Once I start on those nights, it's like I'm watching myself from the outside, telling myself to put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. \n\nBut I mean, a lot of people overeat sometimes when they're stressed, right? It's just a way to unwind after a long day. I always make up for it the next day anyway, so it's not like it's completely out of hand. That's not too unusual, is it? I'm really trying to give you the best answer I can, I just don't really have another way to explain it. I really think it's just the stress, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat at night for reasons other than stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um... no? I honestly can't think of any other reason. Like, if I'm just having a normal night off and I'm not all keyed up from the shift... I don't even look at the fridge. I just watch TV or go to sleep. \n\nSo, no, I really don't think I'd just... do it for fun, or out of boredom, or anything like that. It really is just about taking the edge off after a crazy night. \n\nAm I... am I missing something? Like, is there another reason people usually do this that I should be looking out for? I really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information, so please just let me know if I'm not answering this the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to restrict your food intake or make yourself throw up if you didn't have any concerns about your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, that's a tough one. I mean, I guess if I didn't care about my weight at all, maybe I wouldn't worry about trying to undo the snacking? \n\nBut honestly, I don't really do anything that extreme. It's just... sometimes you feel a little too full, you know? And you just want to feel comfortable again before bed. So, I might just try to be a little healthier the next day, maybe skip breakfast or something. But throwing up? I mean, I wouldn't really say I do that. \n\nI hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is. It's really just about trying to stay somewhat healthy, you know? Does that sound pretty normal to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your behavior of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up is mostly driven by your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess, yeah, maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything, but... in my line of work, you're constantly in front of people, you know? Guests, coworkers... everyone is always looking at you. \n\nSometimes people at the venue even tell me I look a little too lean, which is nice of them to say, but... I don't know, if my pants feel even just a tiny bit tighter, or if the scale goes up by like, a single pound, I get this... panicky feeling. Like everyone is going to notice right away and think I just completely let myself go. \n\nI know it probably sounds silly to worry about one pound, but it just feels so important to keep up appearances. So, yeah, I guess wanting to stay in shape is a big part of why I try to... manage things after I eat. \n\nIs that... does that make sense? I hope I don't sound completely shallow. It's just a lot of pressure, you know? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking the part. Is it pretty common for people to worry about that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I sort of answered that already, didn't I? I'm sorry if I wasn't clear enough. \n\nBut, um... like I said, I don't really... make myself throw up. That sounds so... intense, you know? I just try to be careful the next day, maybe skip a meal or two if I feel like I overdid it. \n\nAnd yeah, I suppose the fear of gaining weight is a big part of why I do that. It's just... the pressure of the job, and wanting to look presentable for the guests. I really don't want to seem like I'm obsessed with my looks, I just want to do my job well. \n\nI hope that answers your question? I'm trying my best to give you the information you need, so please let me know if I'm not explaining things right. I just want to make sure we're on the same page.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during these episodes of excessive eating?\n\nPatient: Um... control? Well... I mean, I guess it depends on how you look at it. I try to be good, I really do. But... \n\nSometimes, on those nights when I do end up eating a bit more... it's almost like... like I'm just sort of watching myself do it? It's weird to explain. I'll be standing there in the kitchen, and in my head, I'm telling myself, \"Okay, that's enough, you should really put the food away now.\" But... my hands just sort of keep going, opening containers and wrappers until... well, until it's all gone. \n\nIt sounds terrible when I say it out loud, doesn't it? I'm really sorry, I know I shouldn't let myself get like that. It's just... it's been a bit stressful lately. I hope you don't think I'm completely lacking willpower or anything. I usually have really good self-control! It's just those specific moments... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you."
  }
